# Export Segments to Meta Ads

Export AIRIS segments to Meta Ads audiences

## 

1\. Create a Meta connection

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Managed ad accounts
> 
> This step is intended for users who manage their own ad account.
> 
> If your ad account is managed by a third-party, for example, an ad agency, please contact your customer success manager to add the Meta connection instead.

### 

1\. Add Appier as a business partner in Meta Ads Manager

  1. [Add Appier as a business partner](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/1717412048538897?id=2190812977867143) using the following business ID:`1546643532281115`.
  2. [Grant **Partial Access** permissions](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/299504287548592) to Appier.
  3. Provide [Appier Support](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#e1849292be929491918e9395a1809191888493cf828e8c) with your business ID and ad account ID to enable the integration and complete the setup process.



### 

2\. Create a new connection in AIRIS

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Managed ad accounts
> 
> Segment can only be exported to the ad account which the authorized Meta account has access to—if your ad account is managed by a third-party, please contact your customer success manager to enable segment exports.

Go to the AIRIS integration page and select **Meta** from the integration list. Go to the **Connect** tab, then click **\+ New Connection**.

![](https://files.readme.io/2cf1cd4c0dcf92c26fdfef031df314435e27a2daec79e842d26b5a9290d0265b-1-meta-add-connection.png)

Enter a name for the connection, then click **Authorize Meta** to connect your Meta account.

![](https://files.readme.io/332add25d54993a0237b09f01114d2a99f7539ba0717928693c064b0756d395e-2-meta-authorize.png)

After adding the connection, you'll be able to export AIRIS segments to Meta.

* * *

## 

2\. Export a segment

> 🚧
> 
> ### 
> 
> Maximum segment size
> 
> Exports are only supported for segments containing up to 5 million users. If you need to export larger segments, please contact your customer success manager.

Go to the segment list (**Configure > Manage > Segments**) and click the segment you'd like to export, then click **Export Segment**.

![](https://files.readme.io/a81f699a1e4647eb4765d0074fe273048877cfe0bb53b8b99422e61ec5102627-export-segment.png)

In the panel that opens on the right, complete the following settings:

  1. Select the connection and ad account from the dropdown menus.
  2. Enter a list name and optional description for the segment—these details will be displayed in Meta Ads Manager.
  3. Under **Export method** , choose one of the following options:



Export method| Description  
---|---  
**One-time**|  Only export the segment once.  
**Recurring**|  Set a schedule for recurring exports. Please note the following:• Recurring segment exports count towards your project's export quota. The export quota includes both scheduled batches and recurring segment exports.• It can take 1-2 days for audience size changes to be reflected in Meta Ads Manager.  
  
  


  4. Under **Export fields** choose what data should be exported. Click **\+ Add Fields** to add more data, then map each Meta field to the corresponding AIRIS fields.



> 👍
> 
> You can [review export logs](doc:manage-segments-export-a-segment#review-export-logs) to ensure segments are being exported properly.

# Meta Conversions API

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Beta feature
> 
> The Meta Conversion API integration is a beta feature. Contact your customer success manager to learn more.

The following guide describes how to create a Meta Ads connection via Meta Conversions API, allowing you to send web, app, and offline events directly to Meta, allowing you to send events without being impacted by browser tracking restrictions. This trigger works for unidentified users without emails, but the event must be triggered by a client-side tracking event such as a page view.

  1. Add Appier as a business partner in Meta Ads Manager
  2. Retrieve your Meta Pixel ID
  3. Create an AIRIS trigger
  4. Check that events are successfully sent via Conversions API
  5. Monitor Conversions API events
  6. Create an audience in Meta ads
  7. (Optional) Create a lookalike audience in Meta ads



* * *

## 

1\. Add Appier as a business partner in Meta Ads Manager

  1. [Grant Appier (business ID`1546643532281115`) access](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/1717412048538897?id=2190812977867143) to the following asset types.
     1. **Datasets**
     2. **Apps** (only required if you'll be sending app data)
  2. After giving Appier access to your business assets, contact [Appier Support](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#f2978181ad818782829d8086b29382829b9780dc919d9f) to complete the setup process.



* * *

## 

2\. Retrieve your Meta Pixel ID

The pixel ID will be required to create the AIRIS trigger.

  1. In Meta Ads Manager, navigate to **Events Manager** , then go to **Data Sources**.

![](https://files.readme.io/c542dd1-meta-pixel-1.png)

  2. Copy the ID of the pixel you'd like to use.

![](https://files.readme.io/d31e4fe-meta-pixel-id.png)

* * *

## 

3\. Create an AIRIS trigger

  1. Create a trigger on the AIRIS console. Go to **Triggers** and click **\+ New Trigger**.
  2. Click **Any Event** to select when to trigger the event to Meta. If you want the event to be sent to Meta when the users do any event, select **Any Event**.
  3. Click **Add Target** and select **Track Custom Events in Meta (Conversions API)**.
  4. Under **Pixel ID** , input your pixel ID.

![](https://files.readme.io/d6f60f6-target-action-settings.png)

  5. Under **Event Name** , add an event name. This is the event name you will see on Meta. After completing all the trigger settings, click **Create**.

![](https://files.readme.io/1b50eeecb15be17a4244003a0a8a2cf2dfa06cef60bd1654a2563a3d61d902cd-target-action-event-name.png)

  6. Under **Event Properties** , **User Information** , and **Other Parameters** , map Meta Custom Data parameters to AIRIS parameters by selecting a Meta parameter from the dropdown and providing the name of the corresponding AIRIS parameter.
     1. **Event Properties** : Map Meta Custom Data parameters to AIRIS event parameters.
     2. **User Information** : Map Meta Customer Information parameters to AIRIS user attributes. Meta requires at least one type of user information to match users.
     3. **Other Parameters** : Map additional parameters as required. Note that AIRIS automatically includes `event_time`, `action_source`, and the required app data parameters, so you don't need to manually add mappings for these parameters.



> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
>   * If you'd like to add Meta parameters, you can refer to the [Meta Conversions API server event reference](https://developers.facebook.com/docs/marketing-api/conversions-api/parameters/server-event) for detailed descriptions of each parameter.
>   * See Meta’s [best practices for increasing matching quality](https://developers.facebook.com/docs/marketing-api/conversions-api/best-practices) when configuring Meta parameter mappings.
> 


![](https://files.readme.io/c51e8ca41306058f24648eb7e36829c720343c2b46a6aa4af7a7a6da4286e5b4-meta-event-properties.gif)   


  7. Click **Create** to save the trigger.



* * *

## 

4\. Check that events are successfully sent via Conversions API

After completing the setup steps and creating an AIRIS trigger, check that events are being correctly sent.

  1. Go to **Meta Events Manager** , navigate to **Data Sources** , and click the pixel you used.
  2. Trigger the event and you should see the event listed under the **Events** section on Meta.

![](https://files.readme.io/b918c80-meta_event_verify.png)   


* * *

## 

5\. Monitor Conversion API events

In Meta Events Manager, monitor your [Event Match Quality Score](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/765081237991954?id=818859032317965)—we recommend aiming for a score of at least 6/10.

![](https://files.readme.io/52b4cc18c6a67956728ce151fa21367b9029c45251041de79f9483b6bcd68e40-event-match-quality-score.png)

To improve a low score, you can:

  * [Import](doc:data-loader-overview) first-party data to AIRIS, like [ClickID and the `_fbc` and `_fpc` parameters](https://developers.facebook.com/docs/marketing-api/conversions-api/parameters/fbp-and-fbc/).
  * Send additional parameters recommended by Meta to see additional conversions reported.

![](https://files.readme.io/bbd54b9d43a1abc9d5a79d034eda2ffb6a8269a59d664a030bcafdcbc5e36da0-event-coverage.png)   


> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Next steps
> 
> After setting up the Meta Ads (Meta Pixel) integration, you can use AIRIS data to create [custom audiences](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/1474662202748341?id=2469097953376494) and [lookalike audiences](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/465262276878947?id=401668390442328) in Meta Ads Manager.

__Updated 8 months ago

# Meta Pixel

The following guide describes how to create a Meta Ads connection via Meta Pixel, allowing you to use triggers to sends user events to Meta. This trigger works for unidentified users without emails, but the event must be triggered by a client-side tracking event such as a page view.

  1. Retrieve your Meta Pixel ID
  2. Create an AIRIS trigger
  3. Create an audience in Meta ads
  4. (Optional) Create a lookalike audience in Meta ads



* * *

## 

1\. Retrieve your Meta Pixel ID

The pixel ID will be required to create the AIRIS trigger.

  1. In Meta Ads Manager, navigate to **Events Manager** , then go to **Data Sources**.

![](https://files.readme.io/c542dd1-meta-pixel-1.png)

  2. Copy the ID of the pixel you'd like to use.

![](https://files.readme.io/d31e4fe-meta-pixel-id.png)

* * *

## 

2\. Create an AIRIS trigger

  1. Create a trigger on the AIRIS console. Navigate to the **Automate** section and go to **Triggers** , then click **\+ New Trigger**.

![](https://files.readme.io/571fc34-create-new-trigger.png)

  2. Click **Any Event** to select when to trigger the event to Meta. If you want the event to be sent to Meta when the users do any event, select **Any Event**.

![](https://files.readme.io/9f1ac7e-Screen_Shot_2024-02-27_at_10.55.45_AM.png)

  3. Click **Add Target** and select **Track Custom Events in Facebook**.

![](https://files.readme.io/e5d4dd1-add-target-facebook.png)

  4. Under **Pixel ID** , input the pixel ID you retrieved in the previous step.

![](https://files.readme.io/d6f60f6-target-action-settings.png)

  5. Under **Event Name** , add an event name. This is the event name you will see on Meta. After completing all the trigger settings, click **Create**.

![](https://files.readme.io/9f5bfe0-Screen_Shot_2024-02-27_at_10.49.45_AM.png)

  6. To see if the event is being sent to Meta successfully, go to Meta Events Manager, navigate to **Data Sources** , and click the pixel you used. Trigger the event and you should see the event listed under the **Events** section on Meta.

![](https://files.readme.io/b918c80-meta_event_verify.png)   


> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Next steps
> 
> After setting up the Meta Ads (Meta Pixel) integration, you can use AIRIS data to create [custom audiences](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/1474662202748341?id=2469097953376494) and [lookalike audiences](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/465262276878947?id=401668390442328) in Meta Ads Manager.

# SFTP

## 

Overview

The SSH File Transfer Protocol (SFTP) is a network protocol used for transferring files securely. SFTP can be used to host files of any format or size, and requires a client that supports the SFTP protocol to transfer files. AIRIS supports the ingestion of CSV-formatted files hosted on an SFTP server.

### 

Limitations

  * Only CSV files are supported for SFTP connection imports.
  * To ensure efficient processing, we advise keeping each CSV file under 100,000 rows.



* * *

## 

Integration guide

  1. From the top menu, go to **Configure** , then from the left menu, go to **Install > Integrations**.

![](https://files.readme.io/210c5ca-sftp-navigation.png)

  2. Go to the **Connect** tab, then click **\+ New Connection**.

![](https://files.readme.io/599cd5e-sftp-new-connection.png)

  3. In the settings panel that opens on the right, enter a name for the connection, then complete the following settings.



Setting| Description  
---|---  
**Hostname**|  The SFTP hostname.  
**Port**|  The SFTP host port number.  
**Path**|  The file path of the directory where files will be uploaded to or from which they will be downloaded.  
**Username**|  The SFTP account's username.  
**Authentication Type: Password**|  The SFTP account's password.  
**Authentication Type: Private Key**|  The SFTP account's SSH private key.• Only PEM keys are supported (`ssh-keygen -m PEM -t rsa`).  
• Enter the entire private key, including the header and footer, i.e. `-----BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY-----` and `-----END RSA PRIVATE KEY-----`  
  
![](https://files.readme.io/3490685-sftp-settings.png)

  4. Click **Connect to SFTP** to finish creating the connection.

![](https://files.readme.io/8d91a9f-sftp-connect-to-sftp.png)

__Updated about 1 year ago

# TikTok Ads

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Beta feature
> 
> Contact your customer success manager to learn more about this feature.

Connect AIRIS with TikTok Ads to sync your customer segments as custom audiences in [TikTok Ads Manager](https://ads.tiktok.com/i18n/login/). Use your AIRIS audience data for targeted advertising campaigns that reach users with relevant content and improve performance.

* * *

## 

Connect AIRIS with TikTok Ads

Before you begin, ensure that you have:

  * A TikTok Business Center account
  * An active TikTok Pixel



If you don't have these, refer to the following TikTok guides to create them:

  * [Create a TikTok Business Center Account](https://ads.tiktok.com/help/article/create-tiktok-business-center?lang=en)
  * [Get started with TikTok Pixel](https://ads.tiktok.com/help/article/get-started-pixel)



### 

1\. Add Appier as a business partner on TikTok Ads

  1. [Add the business partner](https://ads.tiktok.com/help/article/invite-partners-into-tiktok-business-center?lang=en) in your TikTok Business Center using the business ID `7237007394415591426`. The partner name will appear as "AIXON".
  2. Grant **Admin** permissions to Appier.
  3. [Share Pixel permissions with Appier](https://ads.tiktok.com/help/article/change-the-permissions-for-your-tiktok-business-center-partner?lang=en). Ensure that Appier has access to the pixel for segment exports.



### 

2\. Add the connection in the Appier console

In the Appier console, go to **Common settings** > **Partner integrations**. Click **TikTok** , then click **\+ Connect TikTok**.

![](https://files.readme.io/2fbcfbc477fbe205c21a4757e0974929ae3581fafa0b231d0a15bf26834448bf-tiktok-integration.png)

Enter a display name and your [TikTok Business Center ID](https://ads.tiktok.com/help/article/find-your-ad-account-id?lang=en), then click **Connect**.

![](https://files.readme.io/b75340dae2f690f2e5b524ea3e92f71f576967843927fcdf4f53b755cabd160e-connect-details-tiktok.png)

* * *

## 

Export a segment

After you've integrated TikTok Ads with AIRIS, follow the steps below to export an AIRIS [segment](https://docs.enterprise.appier.com/docs/creating-a-segment) to TikTok Ads.

  1. In the Appier console, go to **Segments**.
  2. Click the segment you'd like to export, then click **Export Segment**.

![](https://files.readme.io/a81f699a1e4647eb4765d0074fe273048877cfe0bb53b8b99422e61ec5102627-export-segment.png)

In the export panel, configure these settings:

  1. Select the connection and ad account from the dropdown menus.
  2. Enter a segment name that will be displayed on TikTok Ads.
  3. Under **Export Method** , choose one of the following options:



Export method| Description  
---|---  
**One-time**|  Exports the segment once.  
**Recurring**|  Set a schedule for recurring exports. Note that the changes to segmentation conditions require 24 hours to take effect. Any subsequent changes made within this 24-hour period won't take effect.  
  
  4. Under **TikTok Custom Audience Type** , choose one of the following options:



Custom audience type| Description  
---|---  
**Customer file (Phone number, email, IDFA, AAID)**|  Creates an audience by matching a list of your existing customer data with TikTok users. For more information, see [How to create a Custom Audience with a customer file](https://ads.tiktok.com/help/article/how-to-create-a-custom-audience-with-a-customer-file?lang=en).  
**Website traffic (External ID)**|  Retargets anonymous website visitors identified by an `external_id` such as `user_id`. This requires your TikTok Pixel to be configured to pass the `external_id` parameter. For more information, see [Get started with Pixel](https://ads.tiktok.com/help/article/get-started-pixel?lang=en) and [Set up an External ID](https://business-api.tiktok.com/portal/docs?id=1765942670613506).  
  
  5. Under **Export Fields** , map the TikTok fields to the corresponding AIRIS fields. The fields available for mapping will depend on the audience type you chose in the previous step:
     * **Customer File** : Map identifiers like email or phone number.
     * **Website Traffic** : You must map the **TikTok Field** with the corresponding **AIRIS Field**.

![](https://files.readme.io/e4b2899e288aa88d94f4f61e8d950eb55d94ba96deabedfb3958bac5bf8862e2-export-to-tiktok.gif)

> 👍
> 
> You can [review export logs](doc:manage-segments-export-a-segment#review-export-logs) to ensure segments are being exported properly.

# Export Segments to TikTok Ads

Export AIRIS segments to TikTok Ads audiences

After you've [integrated TikTok Ads with AIRIS](doc:integrations-tiktok-ads), follow the steps below to export an AIRIS [segment](doc:segments) to TikTok Ads.

  1. Go to the segment list (**Configure > Manage > Segments**).
  2. Click the segment you'd like to export, then click **Export Segment**.

![](https://files.readme.io/a81f699a1e4647eb4765d0074fe273048877cfe0bb53b8b99422e61ec5102627-export-segment.png)

In the export panel, configure these settings:

  1. Select the connection and ad account from the dropdown menus.
  2. Enter a segment name that will be displayed on TikTok Ads.
  3. Under **Export Method** , choose one of the following options:



Export method| Description  
---|---  
**One-time**|  Exports the segment once.  
**Recurring**|  Set a schedule for recurring exports. Note the following:   
• Changes to segmentation conditions take 24 hours to apply. Additional changes during this time will be rejected.   
• Any subsequent changes made within 24 hours of the initial change will be rejected.  
  
  4. Choose one of the **TikTok Custom Audience Type** :



Custom audience type| Description  
---|---  
**Customer file (Phone number, email, IDFA, AAID)**|  Creates an audience by matching a list of your existing customer data with TikTok users.  
**Website traffic (External ID)**|  Retargets anonymous website visitors identified by an `external_id` such as `aiqua_uid`. This requires your TikTok Pixel to be configured to pass the `external_id` parameter.  
  
  * For more details, refer to the following TikTok's guides:
    * [Introduction to create a custom file audience](https://ads.tiktok.com/help/article/how-to-create-a-custom-audience-with-a-customer-file?lang=en)
    * [Introduction to get started with Pixel](https://ads.tiktok.com/help/article/get-started-pixel?lang=en)
    * [Introduction to set up an External ID](https://business-api.tiktok.com/portal/docs?id=1765942670613506)


  5. Under **Export Fields** , map the TikTok fields to the corresponding AIRIS fields. The fields available for mapping will depend on the audience type you chose in the previous step:


  * **Customer File** : Map identifiers like email or phone number.
  * **Website Traffic** : You must map the **TikTok Field** to the corresponding **AIRIS Field**.

![](https://files.readme.io/d6fb6d0b1233aa73cad03ca70bb643036388de2cbad2ab80e56d7c796c7aeaf4-Adobe_Express_-_Screen_Recording_2025-08-01_at_3.52.57_PM.gif)

> 👍
> 
> You can [review export logs](doc:manage-segments-export-a-segment#review-export-logs) to ensure segments are being exported properly.

__Updated 6 months ago

# X Ads

Export segments to X Ads audiences.

> 📘
> 
> To enable the X Ads (Twitter) integration, contact your customer success manager.

## 

Before you begin

  * Ensure your X account meets all the account requirements.
  * Authorize Appier as a contributor in your X Ads account.
  * Ensure identifier data in AIRIS meets the hashing requirements.



### 

Account requirements

  * Your account must be [verified](https://help.x.com/en/rules-and-policies/profile-labels#:~:text=The%20blue%20checkmark%20means%20that,the%20affiliates%20of%20that%20organization.) with a blue checkmark (individual) or gold checkmark (organization).
  * Your account must be active, not suspended or deactivated.
  * Your profile must be public, that is, your tweets must be visible by anybody with or without an X account.
  * Your profile must have a picture and header image.
  * The URL in your profile bio must be active and correct.



### 

Authorize Appier as a contributor

[Set up multi-user login](https://business.x.com/en/help/troubleshooting/multi-user-login-faq) and add `@Appier_AI` as a contributor with the **Ad manager** access level.

### 

Identifier hashing requirements

All identifiers for exported audiences must pre-hashed using the SHA256 algorithm. Before hashing, ensure that:

  * Identifiers don't contain leading and trailing whitespaces.
  * Identifiers are lowercase.
  * Phone numbers are in E.164 format.



* * *

## 

Notes and limitations

  * The X Ads connector only supports one-time segment exports.
  * Exports are supported for segments containing up to 5 million users. If you need to export larger segments, please contact your customer success manager.
  * Exports may be incomplete or fail entirely if any exported field data is invalid, for instance, due to an incorrect field mapping, or if data is missing from a user's profile.
  * For an X Ads audience to be targetable, it must match with at least 100 X users who were active within the last 90 days.
  * X Ads audiences are processed in batches that run approximately every 6-8 hours. After exporting a segment, it can take a few hours for the audience to be visible in X Ads Manager.



* * *

## 

1\. Add an X Ads connection

In the Appier console, go to **Common settings** , then go to **Integration > Partner integrations**.

![](https://files.readme.io/6d18f8c84aed6285e828e595531c25aca38d6a609ff4b740d7d03b160b503849-x-navigation.png)

Click **X Ads** , then click **\+ Connect X Ads**.

![](https://files.readme.io/f853187556f4543d287a6e951914d5b1bcbf72a86eccc9ac8a107e755ab2f0eb-x-ads-connect.png)

In the creation page, enter a display name and your X Ads account details, then click **Connect** to create the connection.

![](https://files.readme.io/3ff00369229e876fff52dec11f534533e7bbdcd0873d90ed0b16472c091c699c-x-ads-settings.png)

* * *

## 

2\. Export a segment

Go to **Segments** and click the segment you'd like to export, then click **Export Segment**.

![](https://files.readme.io/a81f699a1e4647eb4765d0074fe273048877cfe0bb53b8b99422e61ec5102627-export-segment.png)

In the export settings panel, complete the following:

  1. Select the connection from the dropdown menu.
  2. Enter an audience name. This name will be displayed in X Ads Manager.
  3. Under **Export Fields** select which ID types should be used to create the X Ads audience, then map the corresponding AIRIS field.

![](https://files.readme.io/4dc4e6a3ce397998d0ee789ec068652194201dfbb3eb1afe42cc0c3a97b0af30-x-ads-export-settings.png)

> 👍
> 
> You can [review export logs](doc:manage-segments-export-a-segment#review-export-logs) to ensure segments are being exported properly.

__Updated 4 months ago

* * *

# Introduction to AIRIS

The modern customer journey is non-linear, omnichannel, and cyclical. AIRIS was created out of the need to understand the customer experience by being truly channel and platform agnostic. AIRIS pioneered individual-level customer analytics even before businesses identified the challenges with nonlinear customer journeys and fragmented product engagement.

The platform tracks user behaviors across websites, through email, social media, paid media, sales, technical support, help desk, video, and most importantly, the engagement on the product/service itself.

AIRIS's journey analytics breaks down the data silos of mobile, app, product, or web analytics, and combines them with other essential touchpoints to help you see the world as your customers do.

__Updated about 1 year ago

* * *

[What is AIRIS: Product Capabilities](/docs/what-is-airis-product-capabilities)

# Journeys

![](https://files.readme.io/f418b8e-Image_2019-03-15_at_3.47.44_PM.png)

## 

Overview

At AIRIS, our mission has always been to solve what traditional funnels can't. Traditional funnels show a linear path to conversion and rarely allow for the exploration of what is attributed to each step. That's where our new Journeys features come in. AIRIS Journeys allow you to map the end-to-end customer experience to reflect multiple customer touch points and dive into what's driving each step.

Using Journeys, you'll be able to map the touch points that adapt to the real-time behavior of your users. This opens up a world of possibilities for measuring where and how users convert. With Journeys, you can:

  * Analyze your user's flow and replace traditional navigation paths.
  * Discover exactly what attributes to conversions as users move through each step in the customer experience.
  * Identify a user's first or last step before converting.



* * *

## 

How to create a Journey report

### 

1\. Lay out a plan

Creating a Journey takes a little forethought. You can break this down into three parts:

  1. **Select a focus** : This is the most important part of creating your Journey report. What story do you want your Journey report to tell? What questions are you trying to answer? Some examples include:
     1. Analyzing the complete customer journey
     2. A campaign conversion Journey
     3. A product usage Journey
     4. A user engagement or churn Journey
  2. **Start your journey** : Once you select your focus, select the starting point for your Journey. These can be things like first-time visitors, initiated setup, clicked on a new feature, or clicked a paid ad. The initial step marks the beginning of your Journey.
  3. **List your required steps** : Journeys consist of required steps and dynamic, footprint steps. Figure out your required steps first and then you can dive deeper into the attribution between these steps.



### 

2) Build the report

Now that you have your steps mapped out, you can start building your Journey report. To create a new Journey report go to **Analyze** , then click **\+ New Report > Journey**.

![](https://files.readme.io/bff78fa923a4ab9c2692fad6437d48c0d333028ecbe62812a7e3a2bb88fc755a-journey-add-report.png)

## 

Journey report configuration

Journey reports consist of four configurable settings:

  * Timeframe
  * Performed by
  * Steps
  * Compare by



### 

Timeframe

If a user performed at least one event in the specified timeframe, their data will be included in this report. The starting and ending dates for the timeframe can be set in the following ways:

  * **Relative** : Select a time that is relative to the present day, such as **90 days ago** or **Today**. This timeframe is dynamic and updates in real time.
  * **Absolute** : Select a specific date, such as **January 1st, 2023**.
  * **Annotations** : Use the [annotation date](doc:annotations#using-annotations-in-the-timeframe) from an existing annotation.



### 

Performed by

Determines the segment or group of people you'll be analyzing. Either select an existing segment or create a new segment by clicking the **+**. For more details, refer to [Segments](doc:segments).

#### 

Performed by timeframe

The **Performed by** timeframe creates a subset of people captured by the **Timeframe** setting.

For example, if you want to run a marketing campaign to target users who are currently active and also made a purchase on last year's Black Friday, you would:

  1. Set **Timeframe** to the current month to select an initial group of all users that have performed at least one event in the current month.
  2. For the **Performed by** timeframe, select the event “purchased item” with the date for last year's Black Friday.



Now, you'll have a list of all active users from the past month who purchased an item last Black Friday. Typically, the **Performed by** timeframe is less than or equal to the **Timeframe** range.

Here's a video explaining the difference between the two: [Timeframe vs Performed by timeframe](https://share.getcloudapp.com/E0uzzAvP).

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> AIRIS supports combining segmentation conditions using the **AND** and **OR** operators. To switch the operator, click on the operator name.
> 
> ![](https://files.readme.io/72170ae-report-operator-selection.gif)

### 

Steps

Once you've defined the timeframe and chosen the behavioral segment you want to analyze (**Performed by**), select the steps to include in your journey. Each step is an event or set of events that users may have done to reach the final step of the journey.

For example, if you wanted to understand the different touch points users go through after logging into your platform, you could add “Login” as Step 1, “Clicked Feature” as Step 2 and so on.

#### 

Required steps

These are the steps that users must complete to move onto the next step. They can be goals such as visiting a signup page or performing a certain event. In order to continue through the journey, users must complete the required steps within the report timeframe.

#### 

Optional steps

By clicking the icon to the right of the eye, you can make the step optional. This means that a user does not have to go through this step to reach the next goal.

![956](https://files.readme.io/648dd19-Image_2019-03-15_at_2.37.38_PM.png)

You can add multiple steps side-by-side (for fixed steps only). For example, if you want to see users who either became a new paying signup OR submitted a demo request before moving to the next step, you can add a parallel step for comparison.

#### 

Step configurations

![](https://files.readme.io/6cb8887d4e46f859a89e0aaba9f364ad9bb53380e809a1a80f5f6e513dd486a7-journey-steps.png)

Name| Description  
---|---  
**Fixed** or **Dynamic**|  You can either add a fixed step where you define the event and constraints, or you can dynamically generate a breakdown of the event.• **Fixed** : When choosing fixed, you can add additional fixed steps in parallel. For example, you could define a specific URL as one fixed step, then you can add another URL as another.• **Dynamic** : Choosing dynamic will generate a list for you. You can choose how you want to break down the list. When the list is generated, it will show you the top 3 events and will group the rest into "other." For example, if you choose to break down by URL, this will show you the top 3 URLs that users visit, followed by the rest in "other."  
**Event**|  Select the event you'd like to analyze. You can also further filter the event criteria by adding constraints.  
  
  * _Aggregation_ * (Fixed step)

| By default, this is aggregated by a count of events of at least 1. You can change this to count events, count visits, or by the sum of the event duration.  
  
  * _Breakdown by_ * (Dynamic step)

| Select the breakdown dimension.  
**Time Window**|  Users who complete every preceding step of the journey including the current step within the specified time will be included in the report.  
  
### 

Compare by

Use **Compare by** to identify the dimension that you want to use to analyze your journey. These dimensions help you compare the performance of segments to understand how they convert through the journey.

![](https://files.readme.io/326db2c-journey-dimensions.png)

* * *

## 

Exporting and sharing

Now that you've built your report, you can share it with your team. You can do this by clicking on the three vertical dots which will open an options menu for exporting, sharing, tagging, pinning, duplicating and reloading your report.

![](https://files.readme.io/ee456a319c52e8afea4d9c3b3e0a5aebabc37a4021e5fcbe8a5fdcfda2c3f91f-report-share-export.png)

### 

Exporting

You can export reports to connected services, such as Google Sheets or Amazon S3, and download report in various formats, such as CSV, PDF, and HTML.

Note that **CSV (no formatting)** will download some columns, such as `average_session_length`, in total seconds. Exporting the regular **CSV** for these columns will download timestamps in a string format, e.g. 3 minutes, 21 seconds - 8 minutes, 20 seconds.

### 

Snapshots

A snapshot is a view of the report at the time when the snapshot was created. Snapshots can be shared via public URL, allowing you to easily share reports with anyone—including external teams that aren't using AIRIS. The snapshot URL will link to a snapshot of your report at the time the snapshot was created.

To create a snapshot, click the three vertical dots and click **Snapshot**.

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
>   * Editing the report won't change previously-created snapshots.
>   * Snapshot URLs expire after seven days. The link will no longer be accessible after this period.
> 


### 

Sharing

Reports are not shared with your team by default, so you'll need to update the sharing settings for others to access it. Click **Share** from the menu and select what team you want to share your report with.

__Updated 11 months ago

# LiveChat

To use our LiveChat integration, you must set up trigger events in AIRIS. These triggers will inject AIRIS code on your page to send us LiveChat events.

With this integration we can track the following: Post-chat survey submitted, rating submitted, end chat, chat msg received, chat message sent, pre-chat survey submitted, minimize window, start chat, show window, and hide window events.

## 

To install

  1. From the Integrations page for LiveChat, then click on the "\+ New Trigger" button.

![](https://files.readme.io/fee88b7-Image_2022-03-21_at_5.38.33_PM.jpg)

  2. Configure the trigger event to match the below settings. Name the trigger, set the trigger to a page view event, and set the frequency to "always."

![](https://files.readme.io/f6d03cf-Image_2022-03-21_at_5.40.16_PM.jpg)

  3. Configure the Target. Click "Add Target" at the bottom and select what events you want to track in AIRIS. Search for "livechat" to see the available trigger events.

![](https://files.readme.io/ea1d931-Image_2022-03-21_at_5.49.30_PM.jpg)

You can add multiple targets for the different events as shown below.

![](https://files.readme.io/8eed54b-Image_2022-03-21_at_5.51.14_PM.jpg)

  4. Save the trigger. After you save and set the trigger live, you will start to see the events come in.

# Export Segment to Ad Platforms

Activate AIRIS segments in ad platforms to power your campaigns.

## 

Add connections

Add connections for the service you'd like to export AIRIS segments to. To learn how to add a connection and export segments, refer to the integration guides.

[ ![Google Ads](https://files.readme.io/a491674fd25412fd061e163935e478a53af470e05d43f98237c3200f48cf6c2f-logos--google-ads.png) Google Ads ](/docs/integrations-google-ads-export-segments-to-google-ads/) [ ![LINE Ads](https://files.readme.io/f8ed27014f391da10cbc6282724ea8196f4d90434ee52aaf9b42db33d122263a-LINE.png) LINE Ads ](/docs/integrations-line-ads/) [ ![Meta Ads](https://files.readme.io/9d6df578183d9d281fe5a66974bf152b02c1e7bb600f451df90204e8f1691ac8-nameMeta_stylefullColor_size24.png) Meta Ads ](/docs/integrations-meta-ads-export-segments-to-meta-ads) [ ![TikTok Ads](https://files.readme.io/85bda685140ca76a07eaa6bde0b6ecfc31a29aed7a0dbc7f9a434a409af45009-nameTiktok_stylefullColor_size24.png) TikTok Ads ](/docs/integrations-tiktok-ads) [ ![X Ads](https://files.readme.io/47604645f4123028df43e061ea4b4a54a866c40b251503cde00b8b04c37be17e-namex_stylefullColor_size24.png) X Ads ](/docs/integrations-x-ads)

  


* * *

## 

Review export logs

To view segment export logs from the past 90 days, open the segment, go to **Export Segment** , click the three vertical dots next to the destination, and click **Sync Logs**. Each entry shows counts for how many user records uploaded successfully or failed to upload.

![](https://files.readme.io/675afe3385770d75f22168e83f388c271f9beba9acb53deb4803ea643b306394-meta-segment-export-logs.gif)

# Overview

# 

Trend Reports

In trend reports, you can use formulas to perform any row-based calculations in the Measure By section. If you are looking to transform any dimensions in the Compare By section, you will need to edit these using a [custom action schema property](doc:schema-formulas).

> 🚧
> 
> ### 
> 
> Trend Reports
> 
> Trend reports are limited to number type operations only.

You can add a formula by clicking to add a column under the Measure By section. Here you'll select to add a formula.

![](https://files.readme.io/b45ba2c-Image_2019-08-21_at_2.50.18_PM.png)

Once you select to add a formula, you can choose which operation you'd like to perform. In the example below, we simply choose to add the People column + 10.

![1265](https://files.readme.io/3f2d081-Screen_Recording_2019-08-21_at_02.43_PM.gif)

You can perform any row based operations and even combine multiple operations when using Formulas.

# 

Profile Reports

In Profile Reports, you can use formulas to perform row-based operations using numbers, booleans or strings.

To add a formula, you can click to add a column and select Formulas.

![](https://files.readme.io/f8b298d-Image_2019-08-21_at_2.59.56_PM.png)

Once you add a formula, you can select which operation you'd like to use. For example, if I wanted to concatenate two columns together, I could use the CAT operations like so:

![](https://files.readme.io/1f6c1f4-Screen_Recording_2019-08-21_at_04.36_PM.gif)

# 

Operations

These are the various operations you can use when using formulas. Trend Reports are limited to Number type operations only.

OPERATION| DEFINITION| TYPE| PARAMETERS  
---|---|---|---  
ABS| Returns the absolute value of `number`| Number| `number` **Number**  
ADD| Returns the sum of all `operand`s| Number| `operand` **Object**  
AND| Returns `true` if all `condition`s are `true`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `condition` **Boolean**  
Array_Compact| Returns an array with all empty values removed| Array| `array` **Array**  
Array_JOIN| Returns a string with all elements of `array` joined by `delimiter`| Text| `array` **Array**  
`delimiter` **Text**  
Array_Slice| Returns a slice of `array` from `start` to `end`| Array| `array` **Array**  
`start` **Number**  
`end` **Number**  
ASCII| Returns only ASCII characters string of `text`.| Object| `text` **Text**  
AVG| Returns the average of the `input`s| Number| `input` **Number**  
CAST| Casts the value at the position `array` as type `postition`| Object| `array` **Object**  
`postition` **Number**  
CAT| Concatenates a list of strings| Text| `part` **Object**  
CEIL| Rounds `number` up| Number| `number` **Number**  
CHAR_AT| Returns char of `text` at index `index`| Number| `text` **Text**  
`index` **Number**  
CHOOSE| Returns first non-empty parameter| Object| `parameters` **Object**  
CONTAINS| Returns `true` if `left` contains `right`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `left` **Text**  
`right` **Text**  
DATASTORE_LOOKUP| Returns the value of `return column name` in the `resource table` where `if column name` is `if column value`. Returns `default value` if no match is found| Text| `resource table` **Text**  
`case` **Object**  
`if column name` **Text**  
`if column value` **Text**  
`return column name` **Text**  
`default value` **Text**  
DATE_FORMAT| Formats `timestamp` as a `format`| Object| `timestamp` **Object**  
`format` **Text**  
DATE_PARSE| Converts date `timestamp` having format `format` to unix milliseconds| Object| `timestamp` **Object**  
`format` **Text**  
DEFAULT| Returns `return`| Object| `return` **Object**  
DIVIDE| Returns the division result of `divisor` / `dividend`| Number| `dividend` **Number**  
`divisor` **Number**  
DOUBLE| Casts `object` to a double| Number| `object` **Object**  
EMPTY| Returns `true` if `input text` is empty, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `input text` **Text**  
ENDS| Returns `true` if `left` ends with `right`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `left` **Text**  
`right` **Text**  
EQ| Returns `true` if `number a` is equal to `number b`, otherwise `false`| Object| `number a` **Number**  
`number b` **Number**  
EXACTMATCH| Returns `true` if `left` case sensitively matches `right`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `left` **Text**  
`right` **Text**  
FIND| Returns the position of `find text` inside `within text`. Return `-1` if the text is not found| Number| `within text` **Text**  
`find text` **Text**  
`start index` **Number**  
FLOOR| Rounds `number` down| Number| `number` **Number**  
GT| Returns `true` if `number a` is greater than `number b`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `number a` **Number**  
`number b` **Number**  
GTE| Returns `true` if `number a` is greater or equal to `number b`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `number a` **Number**  
`number b` **Number**  
IF_THEN_ELSE| Returns `true result` if `condition` is `true`, otherwise `false result`| Object| `condition` **Boolean**  
`true result` **Object**  
`false result` **Object**  
INDEX| Returns the value at the position `postition` in the range or array `array`| Text| `array` **Object**  
`postition` **Number**  
INT| Casts `object` to an integer| Object| 

  * 
  
IS| Returns `true` if `left` matches `right`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `left` **Text**  
`right` **Text**  
JSON_QUERY| Takes a source json and extracts characters and tokens from it| Object| `object` **Object**  
`token` **Text**  
LONG| Casts `object` to a long| Number| `object` **Object**  
LT| Returns `true` if `number a` is less than `number b`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `number a` **Number**  
`number b` **Number**  
LTE| Returns `true` if `number a` is less than or equal to `number b`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `number a` **Number**  
`number b` **Number**  
MATCH| Returns `true` if `left` matches `right`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `left` **Text**  
`right` **Text**  
MAX| Returns the largest number in a list of numbers| Number| `number` **Number**  
MIN| Returns the smallest number in a list of numbers| Number| `number` **Number**  
MULTIPLY| Returns the multiplication of the numbers| Number| `operand` **Number**  
NEQ| Returns `true` if `number a` is not equal to `number b`, otherwise `false`| Object| `number a ` **Number**  
`number b` **Number**  
NORMALIZE_UNIX_TIME| Parses `timestamp` as a unix timestamp| Object| `timestamp` **Object**  
NOT| Reverses the boolean value of `input`| Boolean| `input` **Boolean**  
NOW| Returns the current time| Object| 

  * 
  
NULL| Returns `null`| Object| 

  * 
  
OR| Returns `true` if any of `condition`s are `true`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `condition` **Boolean**  
POW| Returns the value of `number` ^ `exponent`| Number| `number` **Number**  
`exponent` **Number**  
REGEX_GROUP| Finds the subsequence at `index` of the input sequence `input` that matches `pattern`| Text| `input` **Text**  
`pattern` **Text**  
`index` **Number**  
REGEX_MATCH| Returns `true` if `input` matches against `pattern`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `input` **Text**  
`pattern` **Text**  
REPLACE| Replaces all occurrences of `target` in `input` with `replacement`| Text| `input` **Text**  
`target` **Text**  
`replacement` **Text**  
SELECT| Returns first non-`null` parameter| Object| `parameters` **Object**  
SENSITIVE| Marks `value` as sensitive information (PII)| Text| `value` **Object**  
SENSITIVE_EXT| Marks `value` as sensitive information (PII)| Text| `value` **Object**  
SPLIT| Splits `text` around the character/text `delimiter` into an array| Array| `text` **Text**  
`delimiter` **Text**  
SQRT| Returns the square root of `number`| Number| `number` **Number**  
STARTS| Returns `true` if `left` starts with `right`, otherwise `false`| Boolean| `left` **Text**  
`right` **Text**  
STRING| Casts `object` to a string| Text| `object` **Object**  
SUBSTRING| Returns a string that is a substring of `text`. The substring begins with the character at `begin index` and extends to `end index` or the end of the string| Text| `text` **Text**`begin index` **Number**`end index` **Number**  
SUBTRACT| Returns the subtraction result `number a` \- `number b`| Number| `number a` **Number**  
`number b` **Number**  
SWITCH| Returns `case return value` if any `case match` matches `test value`, otherwise `default`| Object| `test value` **Text**  
`case` **Object**  
`case match` **Text**  
`case return value` **Text**  
`default` **Object**  
SWITCH_REGEX| Returns `case return value` if any `case regex match` matches `test value`, otherwise `default`| Object| `test value` **Text**  
`case` **Object**  
`case regex match` **Text**  
`case return value` **Text**  
`default` **Object**  
TO_LOWER_CASE| Converts `text` to lowercase| Text| `text` **Text**  
TO_UNIX_MS| Converts date `timestamp` having format `format` to unix milliseconds| Object| `timestamp` **Object**  
`format` **Text**  
TO_UPPER_CASE| Converts `text` to uppercase| Text| `text` **Text**  
TRIM| Returns `text` with surrounding whitespace removed| Object| `text` **Text**  
UNIX_TO_ISO_TIMESTAMP| Converts unix timestamp to ISO timestamp format| Text| `timestamp` **Object**  
URL_TO_HOST| Extracts the host part of `url`| Text| `url` **Text**  
WHATAMI| Returns the object (visitor or organization) of the profile| Object| 

  * 
  
WHOAMI| Returns the PID (profile ID) of the profile| Object| 

  * 
  
=================| =================| ======| ====================

# Overview

You can set up triggers and automation based on a user event. AIRIS provides the following automation types:

  * [**Triggers**](doc:triggers) can be a valuable tool when users visit your webpage or app. Maybe you want to trigger an email or run some specific script when a user completes a specific task. Triggers will allow you to automate these events.
  * [**Scheduled batches**](doc:scheduled-batches) allow you to set up actions to be taken on custom [Segments](doc:segments). For example, you can set up a weekly email to be sent to a specific Segment or group of people.



To view and create new automation, go to **Automate** , then click **Triggers** or **Scheduled Batches**.

![](https://files.readme.io/5b332b7-airis-automate.png)

__Updated about 1 year ago

# Pardot

# 

Features

# 

Automations and Triggers

  * **Add to List** \- Add this user to a Pardot email list
  * **Remove from List** \- Remove this user from a Pardot email list



# 

Under the Hood

## 

App-Managed Hidden Triggers

  * **Fetch Visitor Email** \- Hidden Trigger to find prospect by id and fetch email from pardot if it's missing from the AIRIS profile.



## 

Scheduled Tasks

  * **Fetch Pardot Events** \- Automatically fetch user events from Pardot on a regular basis.



# 

Schemas

## 

Tracked Events

  * **Pardot Opened Email** \- A user opened an email sent via Pardot.
  * **Pardot View** \- A user performed the Pardot view action. This could be a number of different view types.
  * **Pardot Success** \- User completed Pardot Success. This is usually a defined conversion event in your Pardot instance.
  * **Pardot Email Bounced** \- An Email sent to this user via Pardot was Bounced.
  * **Pardot Unsubscribed** \- The user went to the unsubscribe page.
  * **Pardot Custom Url Click** \- The user clicked a custom URL in an email sent via Pardot.
  * **Pardot Click** \- The user clicked in an email sent via Pardot.
  * **Pardot Opportunity Lost** \- The opportunity associated with this user in Pardot was marked as lost.
  * **Pardot Opportunity Linked** \- The Opportunity in Pardot associated with this user was linked.
  * **Pardot Email Sent** \- An email was sent to this user via Pardot.
  * **Pardot Viewed Email Preferences Page** \- The user went to the email preferences page in Pardot.
  * **Pardot Error** \- The user had an error in Pardot.
  * **Pardot New Opportunity** \- The user became a new opportunity in Pardot.
  * **Submitted Pardot Spam Complaint** \- The user reported an email sent via Pardot as spam.
  * **Pardot Resubscribed** \- The user re-subscribed to Pardot emails.
  * **Pardot Opportunity Won** \- The opportunity associated with this user in Pardot was marked as won.
  * **Pardot Opportunity Reopened** \- The opportunity associated with this user in Pardot was re-opened.



## 

User Properties

  * **Pardot Prospect ID** \- This Field is the person's prospect ID in Pardot. Do not change it!
  * **Pardot Visitor ID** \- This Field is the person's user ID in Pardot. Do not change it!
  * **Last Pardot Inbound Sync At** \- Timestamp of last Pardot sync event for this user. Do not change it!



# 

Instructions

  1. Click install and authorize with your Pardot admin email, password, and API user key.

  2. Configure the integration by setting your property mapping and sync settings.

  3. Select the Pardot events you'd like to track in AIRIS.

  4. Select "Apply Configuration" and you're done!




The integration will also automatically install triggers in the [Triggers](doc:triggers) section that you can use to add and remove users to email lists, dynamically update contact data and more!

__Updated about 1 year ago

# Predictions

> 📘
> 
> Contact your customer success manager to enable this feature.

## 

Overview

Use AIRIS prediction scores to enhance campaign effectiveness by identifying users based on their behavior and likelihood of engagement. By leveraging these scores to create targeted segments, you can drive higher conversions, increase click-through rates (CTR), and optimize the return on investment (ROI) of their campaigns.

![](https://files.readme.io/f0c846d6e47acf90240f223d1fdd7a16dd4848563c9f788d993afca03f3e8d4f-predictions-1.png)

### 

Prediction types

The following table describes the available prediction types.

Prediction type| Description| Example campaign use cases| Target campaign audience  
---|---|---|---  
**Conversion Score**|  Use Conversion Score to predict a user's intent to convert during their session.This prediction type enables precise targeting of high-potential customers, increasing overall conversion rates and customer lifetime value.| • General marketing campaigns, including storewide discounts or clearance sales.• Targeting high-value customers to maximize conversions.| Users who are likely to convert.  
**Campaign Engagement Score**|  Use Campaign Engagement Score to predict the likelihood of a user interacting with a campaign.This prediction type is particularly useful for re-engaging inactive users or driving CTR in promotional campaigns.| • Re-engaging users who haven't visited recently but showed high engagement before.• Promotional campaigns featuring deeper discounts or special sales.| Inactive users who are likely to interact with a campaign.  
  
  


* * *

## 

Creating a segment using predictions

### 

1\. Enable predictions

Go to **Configure** , then from the left menu, go to **Manage > Predictions**.

![](https://files.readme.io/8bdbb13b6d9a9b8c74ad74eb182b4f4d5cebdfd4feab329570abcab77671433b-predictions-2.png)

Click the prediction you'd like to start using, then click **Activate**.

![](https://files.readme.io/f99983bd65a1778ba79b8ec3351926c607034ff421ab897a66bb06b2ec4b5d30-predictions-3.png)

### 

2\. Create a segment

After activating the prediction, choose a template under **Segment templates** and click **Create Segment**.

![](https://files.readme.io/929248e100f665e56d5f88647b03fe536137a43e2e49d7f9b38add69212d8a7a-predictions-4.png)

Next, adjust the segmentation criteria and criteria values to fit your specific requirements.

![](https://files.readme.io/643dd5051acfc97981ff2540f8848f1e33ecc5eac884f6b0d5ffc028f2cf81b7-predictions-segmentation.gif)

The number of **Estimated users** displays the estimated segment size—if the segment size doesn't meet your expectations, you can continue adjusting the segmentation criteria and reviewing the estimated user count.

![](https://files.readme.io/292ae8bf65e1b16e723ac9b210c91748596b5042c015535b6a1de83614bd483d-predictions-5.png)

Once you're satisfied with the segmentation criteria, save the segment. The newly-created segment will appear in the [segment list](doc:segments), and you'll be able to start using it in your campaigns.

#### 

Segmentation criteria

The following table describes each segmentation criteria.

Criteria| Usage| Description  
---|---|---  
**Likelihood level**|  Segment users based on how many standard deviations they are from the average (mean) prediction score.| There are five likelihood levels:• **Very Low** : Scores less than or equal to -2 standard deviations from the mean.• **Low** : Scores between -2 and -1 standard deviations from the mean.• **Medium** : Scores within ±1 standard deviation from the mean.• **High** : Scores between +1 and +2 standard deviations from the mean.• **Very High** : Scores greater than or equal to +2 standard deviations from the mean.  
**Percentile**|  Segment users based on their percentile, or relative prediction score.| Users are split into 100 equally-sized groups based on their prediction score so that each group represents one percent of the total population.  
**Score**|  Segment users based on their raw prediction score.| The user's raw score.  
  
  


> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip: Likelihood level vs Percentile
> 
>   * **Likelihood level** : Ideal for optimizing resource allocation, such as assigning different incentive levels based on user engagement intensity. This criterion can help to identify behavioral outliers.
>   * **Percentile** : Ideal for campaigns where ranking is the primary goal, such as rewarding the top 10% of users. This criterion ranks based on equally-sized groups, making it easier to control audience size.
>

# AIRIS's Profile ID System

## 

Overview

AIRIS has an advanced ID mapping system that allows it to use many different fields to uniquely identify an individual profile. The basic concepts section introduces some conventional database concepts and AIRIS's challenges—then we'll dive into how AIRIS's ID system works and how to make the best use of it.

* * *

## 

Basic concepts and problems

### 

The ID field and identifiers

There is some necessary but unfortunate equivocation in the terminology we use here: ID. There is the concept of:

  * An identifier, which could be a database ID field or other identifying fields like an email address or even a cookie.
  * The value of one of these identifiers, which might be referred to as a person's or profile's ID, email ID, device ID (i.e. cookie), etc.
  * The highest-order identifier in the AIRIS ID hierarchy.



### 

AIRIS's challenges

AIRIS needs to be able to tell which person's profile performed an incoming tracked action (or property update). The problem is that people in AIRIS can exist at a number of different levels of being identified. They could be a first-time anonymous visitor to a website or a long-time paying customer.

Sometimes a person will make a few visits to your site anonymously over a year before they decide to sign up for your newsletter, giving you their email. Sometimes this means that what was previously considered to be two different people in AIRIS is now known to be a single person, perhaps originally from two devices, requiring a merge of the two profiles.

In the traditional database world, merging two rows with different identifiers is a messy business.

  * Which primary unique identifier is kept?
  * What if a database user asks for the row with an ID that was removed?



Additionally, since every row must have an ID, you can't wait until you know that all of a single person's actions and traits are in the one database row that represents them. Another issue is that if you want to track anonymous behavior and even attribute it to known people in the future as they identify themselves to you, then using a single ID value per person becomes more complex. Similarly, if you want to track behavior across channels, then it's basically impossible to maintain the database ID for the profile between your website and, say, your email marketing automation service.

These and other more nuanced issues make this problem of identifiers significant in the AIRIS system. AIRIS solves this problem by dedicating an entire sub-system to managing identifiers.

### 

The goal

AIRIS needs to be able to take whatever information is available about a person performing the action in an incoming track request and use it to determine, with the highest accuracy possible, which other actions this person has performed and thus, to which profile the incoming events belong.

If a user visits your website anonymously, all you have is a cookie, which is conceptually a device ID pointing to that browser on that machine. The cookie will be the same if the person uses the same machine and browser during their next visit. But if they visit from a different browser or device, such as their phone, you will have a new cookie. When that person eventually signs in, AIRIS needs to be able to associate these devices and know that these cookies all refer to one person.

Similarly, you may have an incoming "Email Sent" event from your email marketing tool that is not from a browser and has no cookie. This event has an email address—another major identifier. When the person eventually signs in with that email address on their browser with that cookie they had in the past, AIRIS needs to be able to associate that the events performed by cookie 1, cookie 2, and email 1 all belong to the same profile.

* * *

## 

AIRIS's multi-ID system

AIRIS's profile ID system supports multiple profile identifier fields. The ID fields exist in a hierarchy and are stored in their own database that associates or maps profiles to the values of various identifiers in the ID hierarchy that have been given to a person.

When an individual first visits your site, they will be an anonymous visitor. A unique user profile is created and AIRIS will track them via Appier ID. Once they are identified, their profile will be updated with their visitor properties. If there are multiple profiles for the same user, AIRIS will automatically merge these profiles based on a unique ID and based on the ID hierarchy.

### 

Profile IDs and the ID hierarchy

By default, all AIRIS projects are preset with **Appier ID** as a profile identifier. An Appier ID is automatically generated for all web and app users tracked by Appier SDK.

AIRIS also allows you to add multiple custom profile IDs, such as users' email addresses, phone numbers, or the customer ID used by your business. You can also hash the email and pass it as a custom profile ID.

  * **Appier ID** : The default profile ID for new AIRIS projects.
  * **Custom profile IDs** : Additional profile IDs that are added to your project (e.g. `email`, `user_id`).



With multiple identifiers, you need to define the ID hierarchy, which determines which identifier takes precedence when mapping new user data to profiles. For example, you might have your customer user ID (`user_id`) as the highest priority, email as the second priority, and cookie (`appier_id`) as the third priority.

### 

Assigning new data

Every track request, whether it's a user event or property, needs to include at least one user identifier; otherwise, the user data will be dropped since AIRIS doesn't know which profile this newly tracked data belongs to.

When a user event or property is sent to AIRIS, the identifier is submitted to the ID system. If mapped, the ID system returns the mapped profile, and the new user data is now merged with that profile. If there are multiple identifiers on a track record, AIRIS will start with the highest-order identifier and go to the next identifier in the list if not mapped. If none of the identifiers are mapped, a new profile is created.

### 

Profile mapping and merging

Here's how AIRIS maps and merges incoming user data to existing profiles using ID hierarchy. See each section for details and examples.

  * Highest-order identifier takes precedence
  * [Newer user property overwrites older property](newer-user-property-overwrite-older-property)
  * New data merges with the most recent profile
  * Previously overwritten identifiers can be used for profile mapping



> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> ID hierarchy in examples
> 
> In the examples below, we will use the following ID hierarchy:
> 
> Customer user ID >> Email >> Appier ID (Cookie in web users)

#### 

Highest-order identifier takes precedence

When there are multiple identifiers, AIRIS maps profiles using the identifier with the highest priority that exists in both the incoming data and the existing profile.

In example A, the new data will merge with profile 1 because they share the same cookie (Appier ID). Cookie is the highest-priority identifier that exists in both the incoming data and the existing profile.

![Profile mapped](https://files.readme.io/68d9b03-ExampleA.png)

Example A

In example B, since the `user_id`s do not match, the new data will not merge with profile 1 and will become a new profile. Even though they share the same ` email`, in this example, `email` has a lower priority in the ID hierarchy compared to `user_id`.

![not mapped](https://files.readme.io/7a8a7ae-ExampleB.png)

Example B

#### 

Newer user property overwrites older property

When the new user data is successfully mapped with an existing profile, if the value for a user property is different, AIRIS will take the value of the newer data, which is the newly tracked user property.

In example C, the new data merges with profile 1, because `user_id` matches. Since the incoming data is more recent compared to the existing profile,`[[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection)` will override `[[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection)`. Events will not be overwritten. All events will be retained in the merged profile.

![Rule 2](https://files.readme.io/1efee97-Screen_Shot_2024-01-08_at_4.12.26_PM.png)

Example C

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> User events will not be overwritten
> 
> Unlike user properties, user events will not be overwritten during profile merging. Events will be attached to the merged profile.

#### 

New data merges with the most recent profile

If the newly tracked data maps with multiple profiles in the database, it will only merge with the most recent profile in the database. AIRIS will look at the last tracked user data in each profile, and the profile with the most recent track record will be considered the most recent profile.

In example D, the new data is only merged with profile 2. Although both profile 1 and profile 2 share the same email with the new data, AIRIS only merges it with the most recent profile.

![Rule 2](https://files.readme.io/56326aa-ExampleD.png)

Example D

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> If most of your events are tracked with email, email should have higher priority in the ID hierarchy to avoid the scenario in example D.

#### 

Previously overwritten identifiers can be used for profile mapping

If an identifier was overwritten during a profile merge, AIRIS can still use this overwritten identifier to map incoming new user data that uses this overwritten identifier.

In the example below, even though the email `[[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection)` was already overwritten by new data 1, AIRIS can still map new data 2 to profile 1 based on this overwritten email. And since new data 2 is more recent, the email `[[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection)` will overwrite `[[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection)`.

![Example E](https://files.readme.io/6f4cb96-ExampleE.png)

Example E

# Profile Reports

![](https://files.readme.io/3cdf306-profile-report.png)

## 

Overview

AIRIS’s profile reports allow you to quickly generate a view of the people engaging with your brand, across the custom metrics that matter most to you. First, segment your report based on properties like transactional history, recency, at-risk-of-churn, and engagement. Next, add columns to create a personalized view that can be saved for quick reference and future use throughout the platform.

Profile reports allow you to easily answer questions like:

  * Who are the people that are highly engaged in my product?
  * Who has recently signed up from a Facebook Marketing campaign and what industry are they in?
  * Who is using our latest product feature and where are they from?



* * *

## 

Creating a profile report

To create a new profile report go to **Profiles** , then click **\+ New Profile Report**.

![](https://files.readme.io/e88769c-new-profile-report.png)

After creating the report, you can configure the following settings:

  * Timeframe
  * Performed by
  * Columns



### 

Timeframe

If a user performed at least one event in the specified timeframe, their data will be included in this report. The starting and ending dates for the timeframe can be set in the following ways:

  * **Relative** : Select a time that is relative to the present day, such as **90 days ago** or **Today**. This timeframe is dynamic and updates in real time.
  * **Absolute** : Select a specific date, such as **January 1st, 2023**.
  * **Annotations** : Use the [annotation date](doc:annotations#using-annotations-in-the-timeframe) from an existing annotation.



### 

Performed by

Determines the segment or group of people you'll be analyzing. Either select an existing segment or create a new segment by clicking **+**. For more details, refer to [Segments](doc:segments).

#### 

Performed by timeframe

The **Performed by** timeframe creates a subset of people captured by the **Timeframe** setting.

For example, if you want to run a marketing campaign to target users that are currently active and also made a purchase on last year's Black Friday, you would:

  1. Set **Timeframe** to the current month to select an initial group of all users that have performed at least one event in the current month.
  2. For the **Performed by** timeframe, select the "purchase” event with the date for last year's Black Friday.



Now, you'll have a list of all active users from the past month that purchased an item last Black Friday. Typically, the **Performed by** timeframe is less than or equal to the **Timeframe** range.

Here's a video explaining the difference between the two: [Timeframe vs Performed by timeframe](https://share.getcloudapp.com/E0uzzAvP).

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> AIRIS supports combining segmentation conditions using the **AND** and **OR** operators. To switch the operator, click on the operator name.
> 
> ![](https://files.readme.io/72170ae-report-operator-selection.gif)

### 

Columns

Select the columns to include in your report. For example, you may want the report to display certain properties for each user in the report, such as their email address or company.

  * Field: Display a user property or a boolean value indicating whether the user is in a segment. This is the default column type for newly added columns.
  * User metric: Apply aggregation functions on tracked events.
  * Formula: Build a formula that performs row-based calculations using existing columns in the report. For details, see [Formulas](doc:operations#section-profile-reports).



* * *

## 

User metrics

User metrics allows you to apply aggregate functions to tracked events. To calculate user metrics, add a new column or click on an existing column's name, then click the **Column Type** dropdown. From the dropdown, select from the list of operations.

![](https://files.readme.io/354b688-select-column-type.png)

After selecting the operation, specify conditions to select which events you'd like to include in the metric calculation.

![](https://files.readme.io/95862b7-user-metric-settings.png)

In this example, the column will display each user's total order amount from their `checkout_completed` events.

### 

List of operations

Operation| Use| Example  
---|---|---  
Count| Counts the total number of times the selected event occurs.| Count the total number of songs a user played on your site.  
Sum| Sums the total of an event property that is a number.| Sum the total revenue from a payment made events.  
Count Unique| Count the unique number of times a selected event occurs.| Count the unique songs a user played on your site.  
Last Touch| Displays the last event property of a specific event.| Show the last article a user viewed on your site.  
First Touch| Displays the first event property of a specific event within a specified timeframe.| Show the first product a user purchased in the past 2 years.  
Top| Displays the top occurrence of a specific event.| Show what feature the user was using the most.  
Mean| Displays the average out of a set of numbers.| Show the average payment amount a user spends out of their purchase history.  
Min| Displays the smallest value of a set of numbers.| Show the smallest order amount the user spent.  
Max| Displays the highest value of a set of numbers.| Show the highest amount a user has loaded into their account.  
  
* * *

## 

Exporting and sharing

Now that you've built your report, you can share it with your team. You can do this by clicking on the three vertical dots which will open an option menu for exporting, sharing, tagging, pinning, and duplicating your report.

![](https://files.readme.io/ac5aaf0-report-share-export-snapshot.png)

### 

Downloading

You can download report in various formats, such as CSV, PDF, and HTML.

Note that **CSV (no formatting)** will download some columns, such as `average_session_length`, in total seconds. Exporting the regular **CSV** for these columns will download timestamps in a string format, e.g. 3 minutes, 21 seconds - 8 minutes, 20 seconds.

### 

Sharing

Reports are not shared with your team by default, so you'll need to update the sharing settings for others to access it. Click **Share** from the menu and select what team you want to share your report with.

__Updated 11 months ago

# Profiles

## 

Overview

From the beginning, we’ve believed that people matter in analytics. Understanding each customer's behavior allows you to individualize their experience and sets the foundation for true personalization.

AIRIS’s Profiles deliver a full behavioral history for each customer that's tied to user data such as their name, email address, account size, and any other profile attributes you choose to include.

### 

Anonymous vs identified users

AIRIS tracks both anonymous and identified users.

  * Identified users are tracked by their email addresses or other unique identifiers.
  * Anonymous users are tracked by cookies.



Once an anonymous user is identified (e.g. by providing their email address through a form, signing up, or making their first purchase), AIRIS aggregates all previous anonymous activity into a single customer profile.

### 

Tracking users across multiple devices

AIRIS is designed to track identified users across multiple devices. For example, assume a customer uses the product on their computer, then later accesses it again from their iPad. Most analytics services will count this usage as two different people. AIRIS is smart enough to know that this is one user and will show all corresponding behavior from both devices under one profile.

### 

Custom data in profiles

Profiles can incorporate custom user data such as the customer’s name, and custom event data, such as when the customer made a payment. This custom data is formatted with icons and plain language sentences via your [schema](doc:configuring-your-schema) in AIRIS. Schema descriptions allow you to customize the way data is displayed and aggregated throughout the platform. Schema descriptions can be customized at any time to tailor AIRIS to your business needs.

### 

Viewing and managing profiles

Refer to the sections below to learn about the details profiles contain and how to manage profiles:

  * Viewing a profile
  * Editing the profile layout
  * Editing user properties
  * Deleting profiles



* * *

## 

Viewing a profile

User profiles in your reports contain two categories of data:

  1. User properties are displayed in the left panel.
  2. User events are displayed in the right panel.

![](https://files.readme.io/c7c343e-profile-view.png)

In addition, you can search for properties (left search bar) or events (right search bar) to quickly find the details you're looking for.

![](https://files.readme.io/c1c9c50-profile-search.png)

### 

Viewing user properties

User properties are displayed in the left panel of the profile view and are grouped by section. Profiles are populated with the following sections by default:

  * **Predictions**
  * **Report Properties**
  * **Info**
  * **Metrics**
  * **Hidden Properties**
  * **Unassigned Properties**

![](https://files.readme.io/edbc342-profile-view-properties.png)

> 👍
> 
> You can add new sections at any time.

### 

Viewing user events

User events are visualized in the daily activity histogram and activity feed in the right panel of the profile view.

![](https://files.readme.io/7e8e36a-profile-view-actions.png)

#### 

Daily activity histogram

The daily activity histogram provides a visual overview of a customer's activity.

  * Hover over a bar to display the date and number of events performed by the customer on that date
  * Click a bar to see a detailed list of the events corresponding to that date (displayed beneath the histogram)
  * You can click on **Active** and **Passive** toggle which event types to display in the histogram. The event type be adjusted in the [Event Schema](doc:configuring-your-schema#action-schema).

![](https://files.readme.io/5924f7b-Image_2020-11-25_at_1.56.52_PM.png)

#### 

Activity feed

The activity feed shows every event the customer has performed line by line, including custom events that are tracked.

To analyze a specific event a user has performed, go to the activity feed to see an aggregated list of every event the customer has taken will be listed. Search for the event you want analyzed to return a feed of every time the customer has performed this particular event.

![](https://files.readme.io/500f4a6-activity-feed.png)

Events containing campaign-related data will be highlighted in the activity feed. To see an event's campaign-related data, click on the event to expand its details, then check the **Campaign Properties** section.

![](https://files.readme.io/06478949abeba0f99e261fd0ab7c0af4218172652c5efdee0806db01908e6888-profile-event-campaign-data.png)

* * *

## 

Editing the profile layout

To open the profile layout editing screen, open a report and select a user to open their profile, then click the edit layout button:

![](https://files.readme.io/c72adb6-profile-layout-1.png)

You can make the following edits to a profile and its layout:

  * Reordering profile properties
  * Reordering sections
  * Adding new sections



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Layout changes will be applied for all users in the project.

### 

Reordering profile properties

Click the area to the left of the property's name, then drag and drop the property to the desired position. Click **Save Layout** to save your changes.

![](https://files.readme.io/143a89e-rearrange-property.png)

### 

Reordering sections

Click the area to the left of the section's name, then drag and drop the section to the desired position. Click **Save Layout** to save your changes.

![](https://files.readme.io/007213b-rearrange-section.png)

### 

Adding a new section

Click **\+ New Section**. A new section will appear at the bottom of the section list. Click the name of the new section to edit its name, then click **Save Layout** to save your changes.

![](https://files.readme.io/57c8b6c-new-section.png)

* * *

## 

Editing user properties

User properties in a profile can be updated individually or in bulk.

### 

Editing a user's properties

  1. Open a report and select a user to open their profile, then click the edit layout button:

![](https://files.readme.io/c72adb6-profile-layout-1.png)

  2. Find the property in the property list, click **Edit** , then input the new value. Click **Save Layout** to save your changes.

![](https://files.readme.io/d531360-edit-property.png)

Properties with existing values can also be updated by clicking on the value, then editing it directly.

![](https://files.readme.io/372973e-profile-edit-existing-attribute.png)

### 

Bulk updating user properties

Bulk updating allows you to update a property for every profile in a report.

  1. Go to the report you'd like to bulk update profiles for, click the ellipses (three dots), then click **Sync > Bulk update properties**.

![](https://files.readme.io/08da958-bulk-update-1.png)

  2. From the **Property Name** dropdown, select the property to update and under **Property Value** , enter the new value.

![](https://files.readme.io/930c36b-bulk-update-2.png)

  3. Click **Bulk Update**. All profiles in this report will be updated to have the value of the property you selected updated to the value you set.



* * *

## 

Deleting profiles

Profiles can be deleted individually or in bulk.

### 

Deleting a single profile

To delete a profile you're viewing, click on the ellipsis (three dots), then click **Delete**.

![](https://files.readme.io/7346aa1-delete-profile.png)

### 

Bulk deleting profiles

Bulk deleting allows you to delete all the profiles in a report.

  1. Go to the report you'd like to delete users from, click the ellipses (three dots), then click **Delete Profiles**.

![](https://files.readme.io/9b9f71d-bulk-delete-1.png)

  2. In the input box, type in "`I Am Sure`" to confirm your choice, then click **Bulk Delete**.

![](https://files.readme.io/e788dea-bulk-delete-2.png)

__Updated about 1 year ago

* * *
